## Introduction - Formal Academic Statement

This curriculum represents a comprehensive doctoral preparatory program designed for an exceptional individual pursuing a PhD in Computer Science with focus on Scalable Foundation Models and Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning and AI Systems, and founding the world's leading artificial intelligence company in this domain.

The curriculum consists of thirty-eight focused interdisciplinary programs covering foundational computer science, mathematics, artificial intelligence, machine learning, systems, and specialized areas critical for scalable foundation models and multi-agent systems. Each program includes extraordinary research projects built from first principles, selected high-value academic references, and verified learning resources.

The curriculum is structured to provide sustainable competitive advantage through depth in areas that constitute moat for a leading artificial intelligence company: scalable training, multi-agent reinforcement learning, machine learning systems, efficient artificial intelligence, data engineering, and safety and alignment.

## Detailed Program Specifications - Professor-Level Formal


### Program 1: Computer Science

**Program Description:** Foundational computer science covering programming paradigms, data structures, algorithms, and networking. Provides rigorous systems understanding essential for artificial intelligence systems engineering.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** KareemLang: Complete Systems Programming Language with Concurrent Garbage Collection and Package Registry from Scratch

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 2: Cybersecurity and Secure AI Systems

**Program Description:** Security engineering principles, threat modeling, secure system design, and security operations for artificial intelligence infrastructure. Focused on securing models, data, and infrastructure.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Secure AI Infrastructure with Hardened Kernel, Custom Intrusion Detection, and Security Operations Center from Scratch

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references

- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 3: Mathematics

**Program Description:** Rigorous mathematics including real analysis, linear algebra, abstract algebra, topology, and complex analysis. Develops mathematical maturity required for doctoral research in computer science.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Symbolic Mathematics Engine with Automated Theorem Proving and Lean 4 Integration from Scratch

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

| 2 | Linear Algebra Done Right - Axler - 3rd Edition - Springer | Rigorous linear algebra without determinants |
| 3 | Abstract Algebra - Dummit and Foote - Wiley | Comprehensive algebra - groups, rings, fields |

### Program 4: Physics for AI Systems

**Program Description:** Physics principles relevant to artificial intelligence including classical mechanics, thermodynamics, statistical mechanics, and their connections to energy-based models and diffusion models.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Unified Physics Simulation Engine with Real-Time Visualization - Classical, Electromagnetic, Thermodynamic, Quantum

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 5: Artificial Intelligence

**Program Description:** Core artificial intelligence including search, knowledge representation, reasoning, planning, and decision-theoretic reasoning. Foundation for general intelligence.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** General Game-Playing Agent with Search, Knowledge Representation, and Decision-Theoretic Reasoning from Scratch

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 10: Computer Architecture and Accelerators

**Program Description:** Computer architecture including RISC-V processor design, cache hierarchy, and custom accelerators for artificial intelligence including systolic arrays.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** RISC-V Processor with Cache Hierarchy and Custom Systolic-Array AI Accelerator in Verilog - FPGA Implementation

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 12: Theory of Computation and Complexity

**Program Description:** Theory of computation including automata, computability, complexity classes, approximation algorithms, and randomized algorithms.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Automated Complexity Analyzer and Approximation Algorithm Generator with Formal Proofs

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

| 2 | Computational Complexity: A Modern Approach - Arora and Barak - Cambridge | Graduate complexity theory |

### Program 20: Natural Language Processing

**Program Description:** Natural language processing including linguistics, transformer architectures, language modeling, and Arabic-centric natural language processing.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Arabic-Centric Transformer Training Pipeline with Custom Tokenizer from Scratch - Modern Standard Arabic and Egyptian Dialect

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 21: Generative AI and Large Language Models

**Program Description:** Generative artificial intelligence including variational autoencoders, generative adversarial networks, diffusion models, and large language models with efficient training from scratch including scaling laws.

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.

### Program 22: Reinforcement Learning and Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning

**Program Description:** Advanced reinforcement learning including deep reinforcement learning, multi-agent reinforcement learning, self-play, and emergent multi-agent communication.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Multi-Agent Civilization Simulation with One Hundred Learning Agents - Multi-Agent Deep Deterministic Policy Gradient, QMIX, Emergent Cooperation

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 24: Causal Inference and Causal AI

**Program Description:** Causal inference including directed acyclic graphs, do-calculus, causal discovery, and applications to robust artificial intelligence.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Causal Discovery Engine from Observational Data for Medical AI - Peter-Clark, Fast Causal Inference, Linear Non-Gaussian Acyclic Model

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

### Program 27: Numerical Methods and Scientific Computing

**Program Description:** Numerical methods including numerical linear algebra, integration, Monte Carlo methods, and ordinary differential equation solvers.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** High-Precision Numerical Library Competing with NumPy - Linear Algebra, Integration, Monte Carlo, Stiff Ordinary Differential Equations

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

### Program 30: Game Theory, Mechanism Design, and Multi-Agent Systems

**Program Description:** Game theory including Nash equilibrium, mechanism design, auctions, and multi-agent systems for artificial intelligence agent economies.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Game-Theoretic Auction Mechanism for AI Agents Trading Resources with Nash Equilibrium Proofs

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.

### Program 35: AI Safety, Alignment, and Interpretability

**Program Description:** Artificial intelligence safety including reinforcement learning from human feedback, direct preference optimization, constitutional artificial intelligence, mechanistic interpretability, and safety evaluation.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Complete Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback and Constitutional AI Pipeline from Scratch with Mechanistic Interpretability Dashboard

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 37: Efficient AI Systems and Model Compression

**Program Description:** Efficient artificial intelligence including quantization, pruning, knowledge distillation, mixture of experts, low-rank adaptation, and efficient inference systems.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Efficient Large Language Model Inference System Comparable to vLLM from Scratch with PagedAttention and Quantization

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review
